Furthermore we have used that
D_{mn}(g^{\dagger})=D_{nm}(g^{*})=D_{nm}(g)^{*}
. In the third line we used
D_{mn}(g)=(-1)^{m-n}D_{-m,-n}(g)^{*}
and finally integrated over the remaining holonomies.

The second diagram is the OTOC and can be evaluated similarly

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{I},j_{I}^{\prime},\\
m_{i},m_{I},m_{I^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\int dU_{1}dU_{2}dU_{3}dU_{4}\underbrace{D_{m_{1}m_{3}}^{{\ell}}(U_{1}U_{3})D_{m_{4}m_{2}}^{{\ell}}(U_{4}^{\dagger}U_{2}^{\dagger})}_{\text{particle worldlines}}\underbrace{D_{m_{I^{\prime}}m_{I^{\prime}}}^{j_{I}^{\prime}}(U_{4}^{\dagger}U_{1}^{\dagger})D_{m_{I}m_{I}}^{j_{I}}(U_{2}U_{3})}_{\text{intermediate Plaquettes}}\,.

(D.6)

To evaluate this we apply the decomposition formula and related identities described above and evaluate each integral. We find

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{i},m_{I},m_{I^{\prime}}\\
a,b,c,d\end{subarray}}\int(\prod_{i}dU_{i})D^{\ell}_{m_{1}a}(U_{1})D_{am_{3}}^{\ell}(U_{3})D_{bm_{4}}^{\ell}(U_{4})^{*}D_{m_{2}b}^{\ell}(U_{2})^{*}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times D_{cm_{I^{\prime}}}^{j_{I}^{\prime}}(U_{4})^{*}D_{m_{I^{\prime}}c}^{j_{I}^{\prime}}(U_{1})^{*}D_{m_{I}d}^{j_{I}}(U_{2})D_{dm_{I}}^{j_{I}}(U_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{i},m_{I},m_{I^{\prime}}\\
a,b,c,d\end{subarray}}(-1)^{c-m_{I^{\prime}}+d-m_{I}}\int(\prod_{i}dU_{i})D^{\ell}_{m_{1}a}(U_{1})D_{am_{3}}^{\ell}(U_{3})D_{bm_{4}}^{\ell}(U_{4})^{*}D_{m_{2}b}^{\ell}(U_{2})^{*}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times D_{-c,-m_{I^{\prime}}}^{j_{I}^{\prime}}(U_{4})D_{m_{I^{\prime}}c}^{j_{I}^{\prime}}(U_{1})^{*}D^{j_{I}}_{m_{I}d}(U_{2})D_{-d,-m_{I}}^{j_{I}}(U_{3})^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{I},m_{I^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{m_{i}}\sum_{a,b,c,d}(-1)^{c-m_{I^{\prime}}+d-m_{I}}\frac{\delta_{j_{I},{\ell}}\delta_{j_{I}^{\prime},{\ell}}}{(2j_{I}+1)^{4}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\delta_{m_{1}m_{I^{\prime}}}\delta_{m_{2}m_{I}}\delta_{m_{3},-m_{I}}\delta_{m_{4},-m_{I^{\prime}}}\delta_{ac}\delta_{b,-c}\delta_{b,d}\delta_{a,-d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{i}}\sum_{a,b,c,d}(-1)^{c-m_{1}+d-m_{2}}\frac{\delta_{m_{1},-m_{4}}\delta_{m_{2},-m_{3}}}{(2{\ell}+1)^{4}}\delta_{ac}\delta_{b,-c}\delta_{b,d}\delta_{d,-c}\delta_{j_{I},{\ell}}\delta_{j_{I}^{\prime},{\ell}}=\frac{\delta_{j_{I},{\ell}}\delta_{j_{I}^{\prime},{\ell}}}{(2\ell+1)^{3}}\,.

(D.7)

For di-photon emission this is again
1/27
, so the spin dependent factors of the OTOC and TOC contribute equally.

Thus, in total, the relevant product of matrix elements seen in (
D.1
) evaluates to,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(E_{i},E_{I},E_{f},E_{I}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{E_{\text{brk.}}^{4\Delta}\mathcal{N}_{4pt}(\Gamma_{fI}^{\Delta}\Gamma_{fI^{\prime}}^{\Delta}\Gamma_{iI}^{\Delta}\Gamma_{iI^{\prime}}^{\Delta})^{1/2}\delta_{j_{I},\ell}\delta_{j_{I^{\prime}},\ell}}{(2\ell+1)^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\frac{\delta\left(E_{I}-E_{I^{\prime}}\right)}{e^{-S_{0}}\rho\left(E_{I}-E_{0}^{\ell}\right)}+\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\Delta&E_{f}&E_{I}-E_{0}^{\ell}\\
\Delta&E_{i}&E_{I^{\prime}}-E_{0}^{\ell}\end{array}\right\}\right)\,,

where
\rho\left(E_{I}-E_{0}^{\ell}\right)
is the Schwarzian density of states. Thus, accounting for angular momentum effectively adds a factor of
1/(2\ell+1)^{3}
in front of the scalar four-point function.

Appendix E
A winding instanton

We now considering a particle that winds
k
times around its circular trajectory. This amounts to sending
m\to k\,m
and
q\to k\,q
. Therefore, the defect angle becomes

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}=\frac{\left(\sqrt{(2Q-kq)^{2}-(km)^{2}}+k\sqrt{q^{2}-m^{2}}\right)^{4}}{16Q^{4}}=1-2k\frac{q-\sqrt{q^{2}-m^{2}}}{Q}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{Q^{2}}\right)\,,

(E.1)

where the latter expression is valid as long as
kq\ll Q
. The on-shell action is given by

\displaystyle I_{\text{1 inst + BH}}

\displaystyle=-\pi Q^{2}+2\pi kQ\left(q-\sqrt{q^{2}-m^{2}}\right)-\frac{2\pi^{2}Q^{3}}{\beta}\alpha_{k}^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{Q^{2}},\,\frac{\pi^{2}Q^{6}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\,.

(E.2)

The one-loop determinant for a
k-
times wound fermion can be extracted from Schwinger’s calculation in flat space
[
20
]
. The integral over the position of a
k
-wound instanton comes with an additional factor of
(1-\alpha_{k})
. Putting everything together, we find the sum over all winding instantons to be

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}e^{-I_{\text{inst eff.}}^{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k^{2}}\frac{q^{2}Q^{2}}{2\pi^{3}(1-\alpha_{k})}e^{-2\pi kQ\left(q-\sqrt{q^{2}-m^{2}}\right)}

(E.3)

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k^{3}}\frac{q^{3}Q^{3}}{2\pi^{3}m^{2}}e^{-2\pi kQ\left(q-\sqrt{q^{2}-m^{2}}\right)}\,,

where we have defined the effective action for a
k
wound instanton. For a bosonic particle, the one-loop determinant must be multiplied by
\frac{1}{2}(-1)^{k+1}

[
97
]
. Note again that the overall
\mathcal{O}(1)
numerical prefactor is ambiguous due to the freedom to define the normalization of the ultralocal measure.

Appendix F
Corrections to the extremal energy due to charged particle emission

Another effect of the quantum backreaction due to the instantons is that we see a small shift of the extremal energy (i.e. the lowest energy in a sector of fixed charge
Q
) given by the real part of equation (
5.50
)

\displaystyle\delta E_{\text{inst}}\equiv\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}E_{0}=2\pi^{2}e^{-2I_{\text{inst eff}}}(1-\alpha)^{2}E_{\text{brk.}}+\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-4I_{\text{inst eff}}}(1-\alpha)^{6}\right)\,,

(F.1)

where we ignored the subleading winding contribution by assuming we were working with a sufficiently large BH. This is easiest to see by writing the density of states (
5.52
) close to the edge of the spectrum

\displaystyle\rho(E)\sim\mathcal{C}e^{S_{0}}\sqrt{E-(E_{\text{ext}}+\delta E_{\text{inst}})+i\Gamma}+\ldots\,,

(F.2)

where in the above we have shifted the energy so that
E
is the total energy of the BH, and at extremality
E_{\text{ext}}=Q
.

However, the above is not the full shift of the spectrum. The extremality shift arises from integrating out the positron on the BH background and finding the new spectrum of near extremal RN BHs. There are two effects that must be included to properly integrate out the positron
[
98
,
99
]
, the first is that an infinite tower of higher dimension electromagnetic terms are generated in the effective action. The second effect is that non-perturbative Euclidean instantons must be re-summed to obtain the imaginary shift of the effective action. In this work, we have only included the Euclidean instanton contribution, and so the full shift of the extremality bound is given by

\displaystyle E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{corrected}\\
\text{ext}\end{subarray}}=Q+\delta E_{\text{pert.}}+\delta E_{\text{inst}}\,.

(F.3)

The leading perturbative corrections
\delta E_{\text{pert.}}
have been computed in
[
100
]
. It is perhaps noteworthy that
|\delta E_{\text{pert}}|>\delta E_{\text{inst}}>0
for large BHs, while
\delta E_{\text{pert}}<0
which means that the corrected extremal energy satisfies
E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{corrected}\\
\text{ext}\end{subarray}}<Q
. The interpretation of this shift is that states below this energy in a sector of fixed charge
Q
do not exist at all, not even as metastable states.

It is useful to explicitly write out the non-perturbative extremality shift to leading order

\displaystyle\delta E_{\text{inst}}=\frac{q^{4}Q}{2\pi^{4}}e^{-4\pi Q\left(q-\sqrt{q^{2}-m^{2}}\right)}\,.

(F.4)

The only assumption in the derivation of the above is that
q\gg m
, and that we are working with black holes with sufficiently large
Q
so that the instanton approximation is reliable.
